Well, I think I should introduce myself, too.

First of all, I'm living in Germany. That's why I sometimes (may) have a bad grammar. Anyway, I'm trying to be as good as possible.
I'm now in 9th grade (15 years old) of 'Gymnasium'. Gymnasium is the school, for the most intelligent people^^ There, I'm one of the best pupil
My favorite subjects are Math and Physics. I have a '1' (best possible mark in germany, like 'A' in USA) there. Subjects I hate are German, French AND art. Oh I hate art....

I'm good at school. Really good. But I don't have to work for it. That's cool. It's always the same thing: A day before quiz (it's a lot harder than American quizes, because it's working different...), my parents want me to learn, but I don't^^ And when we get them back, I often have a '1'. At least at Math, Physics and Chemistry. All the others are '2', except French (3).
To get a relation how good or bad this is:
1 - > 90 %
2 - > 80 %
3 - > 70 %
4 - > 60 %
5 - > 50 %
6 - > omg
This is very roughly and changes from teacher to teacher.

In my free time (which I have very much), I play piano, Handball, N (of course) and read books ('Dan Brown - Illuminati' is my next).

Don't know what to write. Ask me what you want to know about me or German things.

If you look onto your highscorepage, it will show all uploaded highscores of yours.

#7032 1 2006-02-24T15:55:52Z spect
I thought it would be very cool if we could make another NTTS.

For those of you who don't know about NTTS:

NTTS is the best highscoring contest ever. The first one (long, long ago) was awesome (that's what I've heard) and the second one... oh well, wasn't THAT bad^^

There are 10 "weeks". Every week a chosen map is played and every demo is judged by 3 judges.

More details will follow if there is enough interest.

So, everyone who is interested in this contest may tell this here.

btw: you can look through the forums for some old threads about it. Just type in NTTS

- krusch (Backup)

The rules should be quite obvious:
- All demos will be made in Userlevels, using the map for that week.
- No cheating will be allowed. Any cheating from any player will automatically disqualify that participant from the remainder of the contest.
- Once you post a demo, you are not allowed to edit that post for any reason. Thus, make sure the demo that you post is the one you want to submit.
- Only one demo per week will be allowed. Any demos beyond the first will be disqualified from that week's contest.
- Anyone can join, and participation every week is entirely voluntary (Although, participating every week gives you a far better chance of winning).
- In your post, please include the time that you finished the level with, for ease of reference.
- It is in your best interest to post your demo late in the week.

The contest is quite simple. The NTTS lasts for ten weeks. For this season, during each of these ten weeks, there will be chosen a pool of ten maps from Legacy, one map for each of the contest weeks. Each participant plays through the map for that week, and submits a completed demo to the contest. Everyone is allowed to submit One demo for the week, and each demo will be judged in both categories.

Demo submissions last for seven days from the start of the week, after which, the week is over and no more demos are allowed. At that moment, the judges begin deliberation, and rate the demos (see below for more infos about rating).


There are two categories in which your demos can be rated:
- Time
- Style


Time will be the Time which is displayed in Userlevels. The top five fastest demos will take the following points for the week.

Best Time: 5P
1st Runner-up: 4P
2nd Runner-up: 3P
3rd Runner-up: 2P
4th Runner-up: 1P

The judges will then rank the demos by Style. This is entirely judges' discretion, but this includes such things as tricks, route-finding, execution, close calls, and so on.

Most Stylish: 5P
1st Runner-up: 4P
2nd Runner-up: 3P
3rd Runner-up: 2P
4th Runner-up: 1P

At the end of the ten-week period, the person who has the most points will be named the winner of the contest.

This Week begins with this post, and starts with 5-4: Rawkstar Central. All demos must be in within one week from this post. Good luck and have fun!

#9527 1 2006-06-17T12:21:50Z spect
You can check this thread for more information.

----

Sweep had an idea for a nation vs. nation knock-out or possibly league tournament.

It'll encorporate both map making and highscoring. It probably won't launch until July, but I'd like to get an idea of the number of countries entering so I can build the format around it.

Basically, everyone is gonna team up as their home-nations. Four people per nation. Two map-makers, two highscorers. Some nations will have too many people for this format, such as maybe the USA, in which case we'll split it South, North, East and West or something. Other nations may not have enough, in which case nations will be combined, as appropriately as possible (eg, Germany and Austria go together). Also, nations are allowed to use substitutes if they decide that's easiest for them.

Each nation also needs a leader.

So, if you guys could say if you're interested, state your nation, and possibly mention anyone else that you know from your nation that could be interested. Oh, and to clarify, this should be the nation you either live in or were born in or belong to in some very strong way.

Oh, and if anyone has any ideas for how to do this thing, please suggest!

#200302 29 2006-07-08T13:57:56Z spect
I don't like all the new ideas for N 1.5

In particiular:

1. Drones going in 22.5, 45, 67.5: I don't like that idea because by using 0/90, more tricks are possible: Going below a drone on a "1" Tile, as in 71-3, for example.
In the end, the idea isn't needed at all. Noone ever complained about it.

2. Making tricks easier, like corner-jumps or perpendicular jumps: These are supposed to require some skill. Perpendicular jumps are already easy enough.
It's like making a slow-mode just for triple jumps

3. Chaingun drones who will kill you every time: Isn't that a bit harsh? It's quite nice if you jump through the bullets, just like in real life... real life.. hahaha
No, seriously: That would make many maps way too hard, including future maps.

#204368 51 2006-07-19T08:51:47Z spect
Quote: Scott5114, July 19, 2006 07:34 am
Quote: chasethebase, July 18, 2006 04:49 pm
How do you add the image like on the other levels?
Take a screenshot, crop it, and save to your disk, preferably as a PNG. Don't resize it. Go to the page Special:Upload. Tell it where to find the file on your hard disk, come up with a name for it on-wiki (usually the number of the level is good - e.g. "88-4.png"), and add a description. Once the file is transferred you can get it to appear in an article by passing the name of it as the "image" parameter for the Infobox Level template. It will be resized automatically to fit, and a user can click the image to view the original version.

If you put [[Category:Level screenshots]] in the description field, it'll appear in that category, which I can see functioning as a level gallery of sorts.

Can't we use NUMA to generate "proper" pictures of levels? There's N Level Data by Eeyore which has all the levels in it. Using the preview image is the best thing we can use, in my opinion.

#12757 1 2006-12-10T18:59:59Z spect
Welcome to the NTTS Season 4!

The NTTS - The N Time Trial Society - is a highscoring/demo-contest and the ultimate challange for everyone who likes playing N!

The contest is quite simple. The NTTS lasts for ten weeks. For this season, during each of these ten weeks, there will be chosen a pool of ten maps from Levelpacks, one map for each of the contest weeks. Each participant plays through the map for that week, and submits a completed demo to the contest. Everyone is allowed to submit one demo for the week, and each demo will be judged in both categories.

Demo submissions last for seven days from the start of the week, after which, the week is over and no more demos are allowed. At that moment, the judges begin deliberation, and rate the demos (see below for more infos about rating).

Judges:
- KinGAleX
- krusch
- spect
- LittleViking (Backup)

Rules:
- All demos will be made in Userlevels, using the map for that week.
- No cheating will be allowed. Any cheating from any player will automatically disqualify that participant from the remainder of the contest.
- You submit your demos to spect using the forum's integrated Personal Message function. Please do not use CODE or QUOTE to separate the demo data. Just copy&paste it!
- Only one demo per week will be allowed. Any demos beyond the first will be disqualified from that week's contest.
- Anyone can join without registering, and participation every week is entirely voluntary (although, participating every week gives you a far better chance of winning).

There are two categories in which your demos are rated:
- Time
- Style


Time will be the Time which is displayed in Userlevels. The top five fastest demos will take the following points for the week.

Best Time: 5P
1st Runner-up: 4P
2nd Runner-up: 3P
3rd Runner-up: 2P
4th Runner-up: 1P

The judges will then rank the demos by Style. This is entirely judges' discretion, but this includes such things as tricks, route-finding, execution, close calls, and so on.

Most Stylish: 5P
1st Runner-up: 4P
2nd Runner-up: 3P
3rd Runner-up: 2P
4th Runner-up: 1P

There's no overall category anymore. Style and Time will be seperated from each other all the time, and so will be the prizes.

Another difference to the last Season: There are prizes for anyone who get's at least FIVE points in one category!

This Week begins with this post, and starts with Cauldron by krusch from the Camisade Collection (see attachment). All demos must be in within one week from this post.

Have fun!

#270814 90 2006-12-15T21:24:22Z spect
A way to get your demos out of your .sol

First off: Make a copy of your .sol and use this copy!

Now, the actual work isn't really hard. The first thing you need is to find the level your demo is on. Use N Level Data by Eeyore or use the userlevels.txt below.

What we need now is the number of frames that your demo has, as this is the only thing which we know about the demo. Considering that every gold gives you 2 seconds and you're starting with 90 seconds left, it is possible to gather the framecount using common sense.

An example:
We know that in demo-x, we take 14 gold pieces and end with 44.425 seconds.
Therefore, the time we spent is:
90 + (14 *2) - 44.425 = Time spent on that level = 73.575 seconds.

Getting the framecount out of this time is easy:
73.575 * 40 = Framecount = 2493 frames

Now, we know that every demo begins with the framecount AND a colon. So, we open our (earlier backup up) .sol file with notepad or another editor (I usually use Wordpad) and look for (Strg+F most of the time) "2493:"

What you do now is to try every demo with that level. Remember: there may be more than one demo with 2493 frames.

And well, there we go. After a few minutes, you probably found your demo using only a text editor and a calculator.

#276695 26 2006-12-24T19:06:06Z spect
The week is over and you guys are doing an awesome job! There are 17 entries for this week.

Time was a very interesting aspect in this week.
As most of you noticed, it was possible to get through the closed door without getting the switch. Most of you did that. What only few people noticed was that it is possible to skip the complete left section and get up the right side using reverse-corner jumps, combined with walljumping and similar. You skipped a few gold with this route though and it was therefore hard to know which route was faster. JF ended up being the fastest with the "normal" route. ska followed him, with a very spectacular run indeed. The third spot though, is held by cyberjuda, who did a very very nice reverse corner jump to skip the left side.
One person tried to get the gold on the left and skip the rest of the left side to get a better score. Nice try, it wasn't good enough, though, RincewindSW gutland

#14782 1 2007-04-09T21:26:56Z spect
I thought of putting that into Serious Discussion because of the seriousness involved, but then I realized it's much too serious and important to make a joke out of it.

What many of you, mainly highscorers, and that actually included me, have been waiting for is N1.5 which, as it was said, is supposed to have several anti-cheating functions, like that Cheat Engine protection that Unreality made.

Seeing as the release of Robotology and N1.5 is drawing closer, we should actually think about how to solve some problems involved, though.

This thread is meant as a serious discussion for all people who are interested and claim to have some programming skills. It's about brainstorming solutions to problems that I see and that other people may see.

And with this I start:

Demos and times can be submitted to the server directly, meaning that no instance of N is running. That's what you see on 0-0 for example. There are two solutions to that problem:
1. A bot that is checking every demo on the highscore board, seeing if it gets to the exit or not, and if yes, at what time.
2. Some sort of Server-Client recognition I have no idea about. That would imply that the demo must be submitted with N, using some sort of signature. The implementation of that feature into Flash and the possible ways to by-pass it have to be regarded! What if someone is still able to hack the .sol or the file where everything is saved?

Which leads us to the next problem, which I see as the most important and most unsolvable problem of all:
What if someone just fbf's/uses a manipulated version of N to get a demo and submits it?
We're pretty helpless against fbf. The only way I thought would be possible is an encryption system. Playing the normal game, every demo would be encrypted in some way. Actually, not just some way: It has to be a goddamn excellent way. If you're playing in fbf, the demo would be encrypted differently or not at all, so it's possible to distinguish fbfed from normal demos.
The flaws of this are obvious: There's always a way if you just try hard enough. Cheaters will probably find a way through this...
